# Agent IDE - Implementation Roadmap

> This file is the canonical source of truth for project state.
> If you resume work after an interruption, start here.

---

## Quick Recovery

After any interruption, restore context in this order:

1. Read this file to understand current state and next work.
2. Read `README.md` for setup, runtime modes, and workflow overview.
3. Read `docs/agent_ide_design.md` for detailed current design.
4. Read `docs/agent_ide_ui_design.md` for UI design intent.
5. Read `docs/smoke_test.md` before changing LSP, Problems, Terminal, Git, or Agent diff application.
6. Check `git status --short` before editing. There may be user changes.
7. Run verification:

```powershell
npm run build
cd src-tauri
cargo fmt --check
cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings
cargo test

```

These are the same commands CI runs (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`). If any of them fails locally, CI will fail too.

## Project Identity

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| Project | Agent IDE |
| Description | Code-centric controllable AI Agent IDE |
| Stack | Tauri v2 + Rust backend + React 18 + TypeScript + Tailwind CSS |
| Editor | Monaco Editor |
| Terminal | xterm.js + Tauri PTY (`portable-pty`) |
| File Tree | react-arborist + Tauri commands |
| State | Zustand |
| Build | Vite |
| Root | `d:\work\agent-ide` |

## Current State

Status as of 2026-09-08: **Phase 8 daily IDE replacement hardening in progress; Phase 9.0 market-parity foundation closed.** Landed since the 09-05 snapshot: priority-quota context budget, CJK-aware token estimate, tool surface on resumed runs, the write and verification tools, CLI tool parity, the bounded repair loop with its UI, the per-run monetary spend cap (9.0.13), and the persistent run message thread (9.0.11). Remaining follow-ups from that block are narrower than the items themselves: prompt caching still sends no cache-control markers, and the message thread lives in memory only.


Updated strategic direction (2026-09-03):

- Based on analysis of Zed Editor, Comate, open-source code models (StarCoder, CodeLlama, DeepSeek Coder, CodeGemma), and DeepSeek Harness architecture.
- Introducing Phase 9: Performance Optimization & Open-Source Model Integration.
- New focus on incremental rendering, intelligent code completion, local model support, and plugin-based extensibility inspired by DeepSeek Harness.
- Maintaining Phase 8 hardening work while preparing for Phase 9 architectural enhancements.

Updated strategic direction (2026-09-04, competitive review):

- Reviewed the 2026-09 market surface: Codex (subagents, MCP client/server, hooks, AGENTS.md, skills, agent dashboard, worktree isolation, cloud tasks), Claude Code (subagent tool scoping, hooks, SKILL.md open standard, budget caps, sandboxing), Cursor (parallel agents, deep codebase indexing, fast proprietary completion models), Windsurf/Devin Desktop (agent fleet, open Agent Client Protocol), and GitHub Copilot (cloud coding agent, agentic code review loop, enterprise governance).
- Three standards are now must-have parity features: provider-native tool calling, AGENTS.md project memory, and MCP connectivity. Agent IDE's differentiator remains the finest-grained controllable/auditable agent surface: per-hunk review, provenance, context budget engineering, and headless run artifacts.
- Phase 9 is restructured: new Phase 9.0 (market parity foundation) and Phase 9.5 (agent differentiation depth) bracket the existing Phase 9 work. Incremental rendering (9.1) is deferred: Monaco already virtualizes rendering, so the real performance levers are code splitting (Phase 10) and memory optimization (9.7).
- Local open-source model integration (9.3) is re-scoped to OpenAI-compatible local runtimes first (Ollama, LM Studio, vLLM) since the existing `llm_client.rs` already speaks that protocol; native inference-engine integration moves behind that.
- First Phase 9.0 item landed: workspace-root `AGENTS.md` project memory is loaded into `AgentContext` as a bounded section, included by default, and participates in every compression mode and budget packing.

- CLI work is now intentionally scoped and first-pass complete as a headless automation runner. Do not keep expanding it into a second interactive IDE unless that becomes an explicit product goal.
- The highest-value next work is back in the desktop IDE path: real Tauri runtime validation, Agent workflow interaction polish, LSP/workspace indexing validation, richer merge/diff review, and frontend/Tauri smoke coverage.
- Further CLI work should be limited to maintenance, smoke coverage, and policy hardening unless external toolchain requirements appear.

The app is no longer just a static UI prototype. It has a working Tauri/Rust backend, file commands, Git commands, LLM streaming, Agent planning/execution scaffolding, diff review UI, and settings for model configuration. Recent work focused on correcting safety and runtime assumptions:

- Added workspace path resolution and path-bound file operations.
- Added Agent context compression modes: `focused`, `compact`, `full`, and `budgeted`.
- Replaced unsafe Agent Markdown HTML injection with `ReactMarkdown skipHtml`.
- Restored a Tauri CSP instead of `csp: null`.
- Added browser/Tauri runtime guards so `npm run dev` can preview UI without crashing.
- Fixed Git untracked status classification.
- Fixed terminal kill path to signal the reader loop.
- Added tests for context compression behavior.
- Added a shared diff apply module with conflict detection for missing/ambiguous hunks, new-file overwrite protection, partial-apply reporting, and CLI/orchestrator reuse.
- Wired Agent cancellation checks through the LLM request and streaming read path.
- Scoped Git commands and terminal cwd to the saved workspace boundary.
- Wired the frontend terminal panel to Tauri PTY spawn/write/resize/output events.
- Added focused tests for workspace traversal, diff apply failures, auto-apply partial failure, and Git status/workspace boundaries.
- Surfaced structured diff apply failures inline on the affected diff cards in addition to the summary banner.
- Wired the configured Agent pipeline into backend execution as role-aware stages: planner -> architect -> coder -> tester -> reviewer.
- Added structured Agent action log events for prompt, planner, stage start/completion/error, diff readiness, and auto-apply.
- Surfaced Agent action logs in the Logs panel with expandable details, context summaries, diff summaries, stage, role, and phase.
- Fed actual pending diff summaries into the Reviewer stage so review is based on proposed file/hunk changes, not only prior text output.
- Added `docs/agent_ide_design.md` as the detailed design document for workflows, context handling, Agent orchestration, and technical boundaries.
- Added backend Agent context enrichment with bounded project tree summaries and Git working-tree diff excerpts.
- Added a compatible structured `agent-changes` JSON output protocol for model file changes while preserving legacy diff/new-file block parsing.
- Added a formal `agent-changes` version 1 schema document, validation diagnostics in action logs, optional reviewer findings, and hunk-level provenance.
- Fixed terminal PTY input handling by keeping a persistent writer per terminal instance instead of taking a new writer for each keystroke.
- Improved terminal startup feedback and guarded resize fitting when the panel has no measurable size.
- Added `README.md` with setup, runtime modes, verification, Agent workflow, protocol, and project status.
- Added optional `baseHash` metadata to structured Agent diffs and reject stale edit diffs when the file content hash no longer matches.
- Added `README.zh-CN.md` as the Chinese project README and linked it from the English README.
- Surfaced diff `baseHash` metadata in the Diff view and added stale-diff guidance when hash validation fails.
- Added Git file management commands and UI actions for stage, unstage, and discard through a file context menu.
- Added per-file diff apply/reject commands and Diff view controls so reviewers can accept or reject individual pending files instead of only applying or rejecting the full batch.
- Added Monaco local code completion provider for stable low-latency suggestions from language keywords, current-file symbols, snippets, and open file paths.
- Wired editor QuickActions to real Agent prompts with active file content, selection, and selected line range.
- Replaced static bottom-panel test/action samples with a Problems panel and store for diagnostics, Agent findings, and future test failures.
- Synced Monaco model markers into the Problems panel and made problem rows jump to the affected file location.
- Parsed terminal TypeScript/lint/test-style file-position errors into the Problems panel for click-through navigation.
- Added a Project Tasks panel for build, test, lint, run, and debug commands that queue into the integrated terminal.
- Added workspace task discovery from `package.json` scripts and Cargo manifests, with fallback default tasks when no project tasks are found.
- Moved common project Run/Debug/Build/Test commands into the TopBar and renamed the bottom task list to Commands to keep Agent Tasks distinct.
- Expanded terminal test-output parsing for Vitest/Jest-style failures, stack traces, and `FAIL` file summaries so `npm run test` failures can surface in Problems.
- Added non-interactive project task runner for build/test/lint/check commands with exit code, duration, Logs integration, and Problems parsing.
- Unified TopBar and Commands panel project command execution through a shared runner/terminal routing hook, so build/test/lint/check feed Logs and Problems consistently while run/debug stay interactive in Terminal.
- Fixed workspace switching for Commands and Terminal by passing the active frontend workspace path into task discovery, task execution, and terminal spawn instead of relying only on previously persisted backend workspace state.
- Normalized Windows verbatim workspace paths before spawning Terminal or project task shells so `cmd.exe` starts in `D:\...` paths instead of rejecting `\\?\D:\...` as a UNC path.
- Kept the integrated Terminal mounted across bottom-tab switches and bottom-panel hide/show so switching to Commands, Problems, or Logs does not kill and recreate the PTY session.
- Added IDE runtime failure context injection for Agent prompts, including the latest failed project command, parsed Problems, recent Terminal output, and recent warning/error Logs.
- Added one-click `Fix with Agent` actions in Problems and failed Commands, reusing the same IDE runtime failure context for structured repair prompts.
- Hardened Explorer context menu behavior so it closes on outside pointer interactions, Escape, scroll, and blur, and clamps menu placement inside the viewport.
- Replaced Explorer browser prompt flows for new file/folder/rename with an in-app dialog and changed Copy File to VS Code-style copy/paste with automatic `Copy`, `Copy 2`, etc. names.

- Added Terminal multi-session UI with session tabs, new session, close, restart, and active cwd/profile display while keeping inactive PTY views mounted.

- Normalized Windows verbatim `\\?\D:\...` workspace paths across workspace resolution and Git repo path handling so Git status/diff no longer misreports active workspaces as outside the workspace.
- Added Git branch checkout/create, Fetch/Pull/Push actions, upstream/ahead/behind display, and conflict file detection in Source Control.
- Added one-shot Git credential inputs for remote actions, remote branch checkout/tracking, and conflict resolution controls for accept current, accept incoming, accept both, and conflict diff navigation.
- Added optional OS credential storage for HTTPS Git remote username/token inputs used by fetch, pull, and push.
- Added TypeScript LSP status details in the TopBar, including server path/source, workspace, install command, indexing mode, detected config files, opened document count, change count, diagnostics count, last error, and recent per-file diagnostics summaries.
- Added Go LSP first pass using `gopls`, with active Go-file startup, install guidance, go.mod/go.work indexing detection, and shared LSP operations.
- Routed Monaco Quick Fix/code actions through an explicit apply command that logs success/failure, syncs editor store state, and triggers LSP `didChange` so Problems and markers refresh after fixes.

- Added frontend Vitest coverage for Windows/file-URI path normalization and terminal output problem parsing.
- Routed build/test/lint/check-style project commands through the non-interactive command runner so Test also records exit code, duration, output, Problems, Logs, and failed-run Agent repair context.

- Added LLM provider profiles with backward-compatible legacy config migration, Settings profile management, Chat-level profile selection, and per-run context compression mode selection.
- Added per-profile model budget metadata for max context tokens, reserved output tokens, and max output tokens, with Chat showing an estimated effective input budget.
- Wired max context and reserved output metadata into Agent context building as estimated token-to-character budget trimming, with action logs recording raw/final context character counts.
- Mapped per-profile max output tokens into OpenAI-compatible chat request bodies and added provider presets for default context/output budgets.
- Moved LLM API key persistence out of plain JSON config and into the OS credential store; profile JSON now stores credential references only.
- Added stricter structured Agent change protocol validation and first-class diff provenance metadata, including protocol, operation, schema version, change index, and rationale.
- Wired Agent pipeline role/stage metadata into generated diff provenance so proposed changes can be traced back to the stage that produced them.

- Persisted Agent pending diff/review metadata per workspace so the Diff tab can recover proposed changes and hunk statuses after reload.

- Added interactive Agent plan controls for editing step title/scope/mode, skipping steps, running one step only, and regenerating a step with broader context.
- Added regenerate-against-current-file actions for failed/stale Agent diffs and hunks, preserving the original failed diff while adding provenance on regenerated changes.
- Added a first-pass Command Palette with searchable workspace, panel, Agent mode, project command, theme, and focus-mode commands, reachable from `Ctrl+Shift+P` and the TopBar.
- Persisted Agent task session state per workspace, including current task, editable plan steps, pipeline stage state, mode, and interrupted state recovery after reload.
- Added frontend Agent run ids and restored-session metadata so recovered plans show whether they were interrupted and which run they came from.
- Added backend Agent run-id tracking and frontend reconciliation so restored sessions can show whether they match the backend or are frontend-recovered only.
- Added Agent plan step reorder controls and backend step-order persistence.
- Added pipeline `pauseBefore` controls so users can configure stage approval points before the Agent continues.
- Added paused pipeline snapshots and a Continue action so users can approve a paused stage and continue the backend pipeline from that stage.
- Added `docs/agent_cli_manual.md` and clarified that CLI mode is currently a headless preview/apply Agent runner, not a complete command-line IDE replacement.
- Added `docs/agent_cli_design.md` to plan CLI mode as a stable automation surface for CI, scripts, external IDE tasks, and bounded fully autonomous repair loops.
- Started CLI Phase 1 with `clap` parsing, `doctor/context estimate/plan/run` command shape, text/json/ndjson output modes, run ids, artifact directories, stable exit codes, prompt-file/stdin support, and parser-focused tests.
- Extracted LLM provider profile and credential handling into `services/llm_profiles.rs` so IDE commands and CLI can share one backend implementation; CLI now supports `--profile`.
- Extracted project task discovery and non-interactive command execution into `services/project_tasks.rs`; CLI can run shared check commands with `--run-command` and records results in artifacts.
- Added shared backend command-output problem parsing in `services/problem_parser.rs`; project command results and CLI artifacts now include parsed Problems.
- Extracted shared Agent single-step runtime helpers into `services/agent_runtime.rs`; CLI step execution and IDE single-step/regenerate flows now share step context enrichment and diff provenance attachment.
- Added first-pass CLI bounded repair iterations with `--max-iterations`, feeding failed `--run-command` output and parsed Problems back into an Agent repair prompt after applied changes.
- Added first-pass CLI repair-loop command authorization with `--allow-run`, including exact, prefix wildcard, and trusted all-command patterns.
- Added `repair-chain.json` artifacts for CLI repair loops, linking failed commands, parsed Problems, generated repair diffs, apply results, and rerun results.
- Added `agent_cli smoke ide-backend` to validate workspace resolution, package script discovery, project command execution, terminal-like Problems parsing, repair prompt construction, diff parsing, apply, rerun, and repair-chain artifacts without launching the desktop UI.
- CLI `problems.json` now preserves observed pre-repair Problems even when the final rerun passes, keeping failure -> repair -> rerun traceability intact.
- Added Pipeline stage source/output visualization from Agent action logs and clearer Diff hunk review/regeneration status in the desktop UI.
- Added a reusable Phase 8 real-runtime smoke run template and baseline notes to `docs/smoke_test.md`.
- Added Phase 9.0 AGENTS.md project memory: workspace-root `AGENTS.md` is loaded into `AgentContext` as a bounded section, included by default in every IDE and CLI run, respects the per-run `includeProjectMemory` source toggle, and is exposed to CLI automation via `--include project-memory`.
- Added Phase 9.0.3 MCP client: `services/mcp.rs` speaks MCP stdio (line-delimited JSON-RPC 2.0) with `initialize`, `tools/list`, and `tools/call`; servers are configured in `<config_dir>/mcp.json`; discovered tools are injected into the provider-native tool list as `mcp__{server}__{tool}`.
- Added a bounded tool-execution loop in `agent/executor.rs`: MCP tool calls are executed through a `ToolInvoker`, results are replayed as `role: "tool"` messages, and the loop stops after `MAX_TOOL_ITERATIONS` rounds. Built-in `emit_agent_changes` / `emit_sdd_draft` stay on the output-protocol path.
- Extended `ChatMessage` with optional `tool_calls` / `tool_call_id` so assistant tool-call turns and tool results can be replayed to OpenAI-compatible providers; requests without tool rounds serialize exactly as before.
- Added MCP Tauri commands (`get_mcp_config`, `save_mcp_config`, `discover_mcp_tools`, `get_mcp_tools`, `call_mcp_tool`, `disconnect_mcp_servers`) and a Settings-panel MCP section for server management, discovery status, and the exposed tool list.
- MCP tool calls and discovery emit `agent-action-log` entries (`mcp_tool_call`, `mcp_discovery`) so external tool use is auditable in the Logs panel alongside diffs and stages.
- Surfaced the AGENTS.md project-memory toggle in the ChatView context-source chips, wired to the backend `project_memory` estimate section.
- Fixed pre-existing `npm run build` type errors in `src/hooks/useIntelligentCompletion.ts` and `src/components/editor/EditorContainer.tsx` that were blocking frontend verification.
- Added `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (Phase 10.1): every push to `main` and every PR runs frontend typecheck/build/vitest plus Rust `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo clippy --no-default-features --all-targets -- -D warnings`, and `cargo test --no-default-features`. This is the gate that was missing when the frontend build silently broke on `main`.
- Made the clippy gate real: reformatted with `rustfmt` and fixed 35 lint findings (unused imports/variables, unreachable feature-gated code, redundant casts and clones, derivable `Default` impls, `sort_by` → `sort_by_key`, `ModelType::to_string` → `Display`). Two crate-level allows remain with written justification in `src-tauri/src/lib.rs`; `clippy::await_holding_lock` is allowed only on the two test modules that serialize env-var mutation.
- Added `.gitattributes` pinning `src-tauri/gen/schemas/*.json` to LF so a Windows build stops producing CRLF-only diffs on every `cargo check`.
- Fixed the ROADMAP phase numbering: Phase 10/11/12 task ids were off by one (Phase 10 items were numbered `9.x`, colliding with real Phase 9 items).
- Added MCP tool approval enforcement (first slice of Phase 9.0.4): `McpToolPolicy` (`deny` / `auto_approved_only` / `allow_all`) gates both which tools are injected into the model's tool list and every `tools/call` invocation. Per-server `autoApprove` lists live in `mcp.json` and are editable per tool in the Settings MCP panel. Unknown or missing policy values fall back to `auto_approved_only`, so a typo cannot open everything up.
- Wired the policy to the existing Agent permission preset: `allowCommandRun` maps to `allow_all`, otherwise only auto-approved tools are exposed. MCP tools are external process execution, so they follow the command-execution permission rather than getting their own implicit grant.
- Made diff staleness detection actually work (Phase 10.7 first slice). `baseHash` was previously supplied only by the model, which cannot know Agent IDE's hash function — so the check was effectively dead: either absent (no protection) or wrong (always rejected). The backend now stamps `baseHash` from the real file content at diff-generation time and discards the model-supplied value.
- Replaced `DefaultHasher` with a hand-written FNV-1a 64 for `content_hash`, pinned by standard test vectors. The standard library explicitly does not guarantee `DefaultHasher` stability across Rust releases, and these hashes are persisted per workspace and re-validated after restart — an unstable hash would have silently marked every saved diff as stale after a toolchain upgrade.
- Kept sequential review working alongside mandatory stamping: `apply_pending_diffs` tracks the files it wrote within one batch and skips the base-hash check for them (the change came from us, not from outside), and `restamp_applied_files` refreshes the stamp on still-pending/partial diffs for files that were just written. External edits between generation and apply are still rejected.
- Enforced `allowFileCreate` in the backend for Auto-mode auto-apply: new-file diffs are held as `pending` for human review instead of being written, the run ends in `waiting_user` rather than `done`, and the action log records which files were held. Editing existing files is unaffected. The permission arrives per run on `SendPromptRequest` and defaults to false when absent.
- File-create classification uses hunk shape (all hunks have empty `original`), matching what the apply path actually does, rather than `provenance.operation` — that field is `"unknown"` for every backend-generated diff, so keying a permission check on it would leave a bypass.
- Scoped the remaining permission toggles honestly after auditing every backend write path: `allowFileDelete`, `allowCommandRun`, and `allowGitActions` have no Agent-reachable path in the desktop app today. There is no delete code in the diff apply path at all (`executor::normalized_operation` maps `"delete"` to `"unknown"`), Agent runs never call `run_project_command`, and every mutating Git command is user-initiated UI. The Agent's only execution surface is MCP tools, already gated by `McpToolPolicy`. Adding backend checks for those three would be theater; they stay frontend UX hints until an Agent-reachable path exists.
- Ran the first real headless end-to-end verification (not just unit tests) with `agent_cli` against a throwaway git workspace and the `mock://` provider: `doctor` reports its preconditions correctly (missing LLM env, then missing Git repo, then OK), `run --apply` planned, generated a diff, and wrote the file, `changes.json` carried a backend-stamped `baseHash` where it used to be `null`, and `--deny-path` blocked the apply and left the file untouched.
- Fixed CLI policy classification: `validate_cli_policy` keyed create-vs-edit on `provenance.operation`, which is `"unknown"` for every backend-generated diff and falls back to `"edit"`, so a diff that actually creates a file was recorded as an edit in `policy.json`. It now uses `diff_apply::is_new_file_diff`, the same hunk-shape rule the apply path branches on. This is an auditability fix, not a privilege escalation fix — `effective_policy` already treats `--apply` as implying both create and edit.
- Added `.agent-ide/` to `.gitignore`. `agent_cli` writes run artifacts to `<workspace>/.agent-ide/runs/<run-id>/`, so any CLI run inside this repo left untracked directories behind.
- **Fixed silent credential loss (`keyring` had no platform backend).** `Cargo.toml` declared `keyring = "3"` with no features. keyring v3 requires an explicit store feature; without one it compiles the mock store, which returns `Ok` from writes and `NoEntry` from reads. Every API key on every platform was discarded, `Failed to store credential` could never fire, and the failure surfaced as `Credential not found or inaccessible` — blaming the OS. Now declares `apple-native` / `windows-native` / `sync-secret-service`. Verified on Windows: a real `LegacyGeneric:target=llm-profile:default.agent-ide` entry appears, survives an app restart, and the Agent reads it back.
- Added a credential round-trip test (`services::credentials`) covering store → read → overwrite → delete. This is the Phase 10.5 secret-storage validation and it fails loudly on a mock backend, so the regression cannot return silently.
- Stopped the UI from claiming a key is stored when it is not: `masked_api_key()` now probes the credential store instead of assuming `credentialRef.is_some()` means "saved", `save_profile` requires a key when no readable one exists (new `has_readable_api_key()`), and the frontend `(saved)` badge no longer treats the literal string `"not configured"` as truthy. Before this, the panel showed "stored in OS credential store", the user left the field blank, the save succeeded, and every run failed — with no way out through the UI.
- Added a masked "Stored:" readout with an eye toggle in Settings, backed by a dedicated `reveal_llm_api_key` command. Plaintext is never included in the routine profile-list response; it is fetched only on an explicit click.
- **Rendered the Agent error message.** `useAgentStore.error` was set on failure, persisted, and read by nothing — a failed run showed only a `Retry` button with no explanation. ChatView now shows the message above the input. This is what turned an opaque failure into the keyring root cause during the smoke run.
- Started separating IPC adapters from business logic so verification stops depending on the window. `apply_diff_hunk` is the first: the state work moved to `AgentOrchestrator::apply_diff_hunk`, `status_from_hunks` and the review-state rollup moved to `orchestrator.rs`, and the Tauri command is now only lock + emit + action log. Two Rust tests now cover what previously required clicking a hunk in the UI.
- **The very first extraction found a silent-failure bug**: `apply_diff_hunk` built its synthetic single-hunk diff with `..diff.clone()`, inheriting `status`. After the first hunk landed the diff became `partial`, and `apply_pending_diffs` skips anything that is not `pending` — so applying a second hunk did nothing, returned `Ok` with both `applied` and `failed` empty, and the UI showed neither a change nor an error. The synthetic diff is now forced to `pending`. Manual testing could not have caught this: the bundled mock provider only ever emits a single-hunk diff.
- Extracted `apply_diff` the same way, which fixed a second review-flow gap: it only accepted `pending`, so once any hunk had been reviewed individually the whole-file Apply button failed with "not pending" and the user had to click every remaining hunk one at a time. It now accepts `partial` / `failed`, applies only the undecided hunks, and leaves already-rejected hunks untouched instead of writing them back.
- Extracted the reject path (`reject_diff` / `reject_diff_hunk`) into the orchestrator, and it had the predicted symmetric bugs. `reject_diff` also required `pending`, so whole-file Reject failed after any per-hunk review — the exact mirror of the Apply gap. Worse, it hard-set `diff.status = "rejected"` without touching per-hunk statuses, so a file with one applied hunk could report `rejected` while that hunk still read `applied`; anything downstream that trusts either field saw a contradiction. Both now accept `pending` / `partial` / `failed`, skip already-decided hunks, and derive the file status from `status_from_hunks` instead of writing it directly. Three tests cover rejecting the remainder of a partially applied file, per-hunk status convergence to `Done`, and refusing to reject an applied hunk.
- Extracted the batch commands (`apply_diffs` / `reject_diffs`) last, and the same bug class was present one level up. Apply-all handed `self.diffs` straight to `apply_pending_diffs`, which skips anything that is not `pending`, so a file with even one individually reviewed hunk was dropped from the batch entirely — no write, no error, and the "N applied, M failed" summary did not account for it. It also only ever set the file-level status, leaving per-hunk statuses empty so the UI's hunk badges went blank after Apply all. Reject-all had the `pending`-only restriction plus a reporting bug: it returned every diff whose status was `rejected`, including ones rejected in earlier rounds, so the action log over-counted. Both now iterate the reviewable diffs and reuse the per-file `apply_diff` / `reject_diff`, which gets the undecided-hunk filtering, `restamp_applied_files`, and status derivation for free. `commands/agent.rs` no longer imports `apply_pending_diffs` at all — the IPC layer has no direct path into the diff engine.
- Extracted the single-step run path (`run_agent_step`) next, which surfaced a state bug the earlier extractions had already fixed elsewhere: it hard-set `WaitingUser` after a successful step regardless of output, so a step that returned only prose left the UI showing "needs attention" with an empty review area and no way out but a rerun. The step-result work now lives in `AgentOrchestrator::record_step_success` / `record_step_status` / `begin_step`, which use `refresh_review_state` like the rest of the review flow. The action log also reported the total reviewable diff count instead of what the step produced, so leftovers from earlier steps inflated it; it now reports `outcome.new_diffs`. `upsert_step` / `upsert_step_status` moved out of the command layer into the orchestrator.
- Added path deny rules for Agent writes (Phase 9.0.4). `resolve_for_write` only guaranteed "inside the workspace", and the workspace itself contains paths where a write is equivalent to code execution or credential exposure: a diff targeting `.git/hooks/pre-commit` would have been applied without question, and in Auto mode without a human click, giving arbitrary code execution on the next commit. `workspace::resolve_for_agent_write` now also rejects `.git/`, `.agent-ide/`, `node_modules/`, and credential files (`.env`, `.env.*`, `.npmrc`, `.netrc`, `id_rsa`, `id_ed25519`, `*.pem` / `*.key` / `*.p12` / `*.pfx`), matched case-insensitively and only against the path *relative* to the workspace root so a workspace living under a directory named `node_modules` is not bricked. It is a separate function rather than a change to `resolve_for_write` on purpose: the user editing `.env` from the file explorer is legitimate, an Agent rewriting it is not. `diff_apply` is the only caller, so both the desktop and CLI apply paths inherit it and the block surfaces as a normal per-file apply failure.
- Started Phase 9.0.4's per-run cost cap by first making token usage observable at all — there was no usage or cost accounting anywhere in the backend. Everything called a "budget" was a pre-request character-count estimate (`chars / 4`) used only to trim context; no provider-reported `usage` was ever read. Two gaps had to be closed together: the streaming request never sent `stream_options: {"include_usage": true}`, so OpenAI-compatible providers would not emit a usage chunk at all; and `StreamChunk.choices` had no `#[serde(default)]`, so the usage chunk (which carries no `choices`) would have failed to deserialize and been silently dropped even if it arrived. `LlmUsage` is now parsed in both the streaming and non-streaming paths and carried on `LlmStreamOutput`. Every field is `Option` and `resolved_total()` returns `None` rather than 0 when a provider reports nothing, because treating "unknown" as zero would make any usage-based cap silently unenforceable — local runtimes and mock endpoints report no usage at all.
- Added `RunUsageMeter`: per-run token accounting plus an optional cap, enforced inside `send_chat_request` — the one choke point every real provider request passes through, so the cap cannot be bypassed by picking a different entry point. It lives on `LlmClient` rather than being threaded back through `execute_stage` / `execute_step` return types on purpose: a single run fans out across the planner, every pipeline stage, and up to 5 tool rounds per stage, and those paths have different return types (the planner's `stream_chat` returns a bare `String`, which drops usage entirely). Hanging the meter off the client means every call is accounted for. Retries within one logical call count once. `RunUsageSnapshot::usage_is_unknown()` distinguishes "provider reported nothing" from "spent nothing" — without that distinction a configured cap would look active while never being able to fire, since local runtimes and mock endpoints report no usage at all. **Not yet wired**: nothing calls `with_usage_meter` yet, so no cap is active in the running app. Attaching it per run and reading the limit from the LLM profile is the next step.
- Wired the per-run cap up: `maxRunTokens` on the LLM profile, read via `llm_profiles::run_token_cap`, and `AgentGlobalState::get_llm_client` now hands back a fresh `RunUsageMeter` alongside the client. Because the meter is attached where the client is built, every run command is metered and the cap applies without each command opting in. A configured `0` is treated as unset so a hand-edited config cannot lock every run out. Run end writes a `run_token_usage` action log that says "not reported by provider" rather than printing 0 when usage is unknown. Settings exposes it as a "Per-run cap" budget field (empty = no limit). The meter also lives on the orchestrator (`run_usage`), so `continue_agent_pipeline` resumes a paused run against the same allowance instead of restarting accounting from zero — otherwise a single pause would make the cap unenforceable. If no meter can be recovered (e.g. after a process restart) it falls back to a fresh one rather than silently skipping accounting.
- Closed the egress side of the credential deny list, found by auditing for the Phase 10.3 security doc. The write deny list only governed what lands on disk; there was no counterpart for what leaves the machine. With `.env` open in the editor its **full contents** were sent to the provider on every run, and a modified `.env` appeared verbatim inside the git-diff context section — the same secret the write rule refuses to touch. `workspace::is_credential_path` now shares the credential filename rules (`.env*`, `.npmrc`, `.netrc`, `id_rsa`, `id_ed25519`, `*.pem` / `*.key` / `*.p12` / `*.pfx`) with the write path, and the prompt builder withholds the active file's content and selection while still telling the model the path; `build_git_diff_summary` drops hunks for credential files and names what it withheld. `.git` / `node_modules` are deliberately *not* in the egress rule — those are integrity and noise concerns, not leak concerns.
- Gave the Agent built-in read tools, which is the largest architectural gap against Codex-class tools. Until now the Agent could only see the context bundle assembled at the start of a run: active file, selection, pre-selected context files, a tree summary, a git diff. To touch a file the user had not selected, the model had to invent the `ORIGINAL` section — which is precisely where "Could not find original content" apply failures come from. Modern agents let the model decide what to read. `agent/workspace_tools.rs` adds `workspace_read_file`, `workspace_search_text`, and `workspace_list_files` as a `ToolInvoker`, composed with the MCP invoker through a new `CompositeToolInvoker` (the tool loop only accepts one invoker, and `workspace_` / `mcp__` prefixes keep routing unambiguous). They are read-only, resolve every path through `resolve_existing`, skip `node_modules` / `.git` / build output, and **refuse credential files** — otherwise they would be a way around the context egress filter added earlier the same day, since the model could just call the read tool instead. `MAX_TOOL_ITERATIONS` went from 4 to 12: exploration takes rounds, and the real cost bound is now the per-run token cap rather than an arbitrary round count. The executor prompt was updated to tell the model to read before writing a diff. **Caveat resolved the same day:** tools are only advertised when `toolCallMode` is `native_tools`, which used to be off by default, so the read tools did nothing until a profile opted in. Rather than flip the default blind — an endpoint that rejects a `tools` parameter would have started failing outright — `send_chat_request` now does capability negotiation: when a client error names `tools` (or `stream_options`), that parameter is dropped and the request retried once, and the rejection is remembered for the rest of the run so it is not re-attempted on every call. Detection requires the error body to name the parameter, so an unrelated 400 cannot silently strip the feature. With graceful degradation in place the cloud default is now `native_tools`; local profiles stay on `text_protocol`. A degraded run writes a `tool_capability_degraded` warning to the action log, because otherwise the run looks like it succeeded while the Agent silently had no tools.
- Made the built-in tool calls auditable: every `workspace_read_file` / `workspace_search_text` / `workspace_list_files` call now writes a `workspace_tool_call` entry to the action log, the way MCP calls already did. Without it the new tools were completely opaque — you could not tell whether the Agent read anything, or what — and a built-in tool should not be less auditable than an external one. Getting there surfaced a self-inflicted problem worth recording: the first attempt held an `AppHandle` inside `agent/workspace_tools.rs`, which pulled the Tauri runtime into the lib test binary and made **the entire suite fail to load** with `STATUS_ENTRYPOINT_NOT_FOUND` — not a single assertion failure, the process could not start. The fix is the same layering rule already applied to the orchestrator: the agent layer takes a `ToolCallLogger` callback and the command layer owns event emission.
- Added conversation continuity, the next gap against Codex-class tools after tool use. Every run was a cold start: a follow-up like "now handle the error case" carried no trace of what the previous run did, so the model re-derived it or contradicted it. The orchestrator now keeps `Vec<ConversationTurn>` (prompt + what that turn produced, derived from diff state so the command layer does not assemble summaries), and `conversation_digest()` feeds it back as an ordinary `AgentContext` section. Making it a context section rather than a hand-assembled prompt prefix matters: it goes through the same budget trimming as every other source instead of quietly bypassing it. Bounded at 6 turns with per-entry truncation — the goal is a trace of what just happened, not a transcript. A run that produced nothing is recorded as "no file changes produced" rather than being omitted, so the next turn cannot assume code was already touched. `clear_agent_conversation` exists for starting an unrelated task; **there is no "new task" affordance in the UI to hook it to yet**, so today it is only reachable from IPC.
- Added undo for applied changes, the last one-way door in the review flow. Rejecting worked only *before* a diff landed; once applied, the UI had nothing to offer and the user had to recover with git by hand — and "applied it, then noticed it was wrong" is exactly when a way back matters most. `apply_pending_diffs_with_snapshots` now captures each target file's content immediately before writing, the orchestrator keeps those as an undo stack (20 deep), and `undo_last_apply` restores them. Details that make it real rather than nominal: snapshots are kept in the backend rather than on `ApplyDiffsResult`, because that struct crosses IPC and would ship whole source files to the webview on every apply; a file that did not exist before is *deleted* on undo rather than left as an empty file; only the first write per file in a batch is snapshotted, so undo returns to "before this batch" instead of "before the last hunk"; and undo re-stamps `baseHash` and returns the affected diffs to `pending`, so the change goes back to the review queue and can be applied again instead of being wedged as `applied` while the disk says otherwise. Wired end to end: `Undo Apply` in the Diff view.
- Brought the verification loop to the desktop app. **Correction to what I wrote when landing this:** I claimed the desktop app had no way to feed failures back to the Agent. That was wrong — `useFixWithAgent` plus the `Fix` / `Fix with Agent` buttons in the Commands panel and the Problems list have existed for a while. What the desktop actually lacked was running *several* checks as one verification pass, and a repair prompt built from the same code as the CLI's. Reading that existing path also turned up a real defect: `buildTaskFailureFixPrompt` emitted only the command name and exit code, while the failure *output* was attached separately by `buildIdeRuntimeContext`, which picks the **most recently failed** task. So with A failing and then B failing, clicking Fix on A produced "Command: A" paired with B's output — the model was handed the wrong evidence. Fixed by embedding the clicked run's own output in the prompt, with tail-truncation (errors are at the end) and an explicit "(no output captured)" instead of a silent blank, covered by three frontend tests. The reusable half of the CLI loop (`failed_command_results`, `collect_command_problems`, `build_repair_prompt`, output truncation) moved out of `cli/mod.rs` private functions into `services/verification.rs`, and the CLI now calls the shared version — same extraction rule applied to the other entry point, so the two cannot drift into different wording and different truncation limits. The new `verify_workspace` command runs the given checks and returns a report whose `repair_prompt` is ready to send through `send_agent_prompt`. Decisions worth recording: a check with no exit code counts as **failed**, since a command that could not start is not a passing check; a command that fails to launch is recorded as a failed result instead of aborting the whole run and discarding the checks that already ran; passing checks are kept out of the repair prompt, because mixing them in dilutes the signal and invites the model to "improve" unrelated code; and when everything passes `repair_prompt` is `None` rather than a prompt with nothing to fix. Truncation now cuts on character boundaries — the old byte slice could split multi-byte output mid-character — and keeps the **tail** rather than the head. That second change fixes a defect the CLI had all along: compiler errors, assertion failures and stack traces sit at the *end* of command output, while the start is dependency installation and build progress, so head-truncation fed the model the noise and dropped the actual failure. It surfaced only because the same prompt was being built in two languages and the two disagreed about which half to keep — the TypeScript side was right. If the caller does not supply the original task, the most recent conversation turn is used, so the repair prompt states what the user actually asked for rather than only listing errors. **Wired end to end**: `Verify All` in the Commands panel runs every discovered check and, on failure, sends the backend-built repair prompt straight to the Agent. One safety invariant is enforced backend-side rather than left to the caller: long-running commands (`dev`, `start`, `watch`, `serve`, `preview`, anything with `--watch`) are never included, because verification runs each command to completion and a dev server never exits — a single `npm run dev` in the list would hang the whole pass. Exclusion beats inclusion, so `build:watch` is skipped too, and the skipped list is reported rather than silently dropped so the user can see what the pass actually covered. **The duplication this introduced is now resolved:** the `agent_repair_prompt` command exposes the Rust builder, and the `Fix` / `Fix with Agent` buttons call it, keeping `buildTaskFailureFixPrompt` only as the non-Tauri fallback. Order mattered here — the two implementations were reconciled to the same truncation direction *before* routing the buttons through one of them, otherwise unifying would have silently reintroduced head-truncation on the path that was already correct.
- Locked down the IPC seam with a contract test (`tests/ipc-contract.test.ts`). Tauri command names are strings on both sides: `invoke("verify_workspce")` is invisible to `tsc` *and* to `cargo`, and only shows up as a runtime error when a user clicks the button. Five commands were added this session, which is exactly the situation where a typo would survive every existing check. The test parses the `generate_handler!` list out of `lib.rs`, scans `src/**` for `invoke(...)` names, and asserts every invoked name is registered — plus two sanity assertions so the test cannot pass by failing to parse anything. **It found no real mismatches**; every invoked command is registered today. It is a guard against the next one, not a bug report. It lives in `tests/` rather than `src/` on purpose: it needs filesystem access, and `tsconfig.json` deliberately gives app code only `vite/client` types. Relaxing the app's type boundary for a tooling test would be the wrong trade, so `tests/` is covered by `tsconfig.node.json` instead.
- Covered the undo path from the store side against a mocked `invoke`, since the feature was shipped end to end but only the Rust half was tested. Three cases, each chosen because the wrong behaviour would be invisible in the UI: a successful undo must *clear* a stale error banner, or the user reads the leftover message as "undo failed too"; a partial restore must report `false` rather than success, because the disk is then sitting between two states; and a backend refusal ("nothing to undo") must surface as a message instead of failing silently, which would make the button look broken rather than inapplicable.
- Added a Linux CI job (`rust-linux`) to start closing the cross-platform verification gap. It installs the Tauri webview/GTK stack (without which the crate does not compile on Linux), runs clippy — which compiles the `cfg(unix)` paths the Windows job never sees — and runs the test suite under `dbus-run-session` with `gnome-keyring`, so the credential round-trip test hits a real Secret Service instead of reporting "no entry" for everything. That last part is the point: it is what would turn "Linux backend unvalidated" from a footnote in `SECURITY.md` into a checked claim. `cargo fmt --check` is not repeated there since it is platform independent. **This job has not been run yet** — it was written on a Windows machine and cannot be exercised locally, so its first result needs review before any of it is treated as verified; SECURITY.md still lists Linux as unvalidated for that reason.
- The Linux CI job paid for itself on its first run, and not in the way I predicted. I expected the credential test to fail; instead the build never got that far: `tauri::generate_context!()` panicked with `icon .../32x32.png is not RGBA`. All four PNG icons were 8-bit **RGB** (color type 2), and Tauri's codegen requires RGBA. Windows never noticed because it uses `icon.ico`, so **the app could not be compiled on Linux at all** — a Phase 10.4 packaging blocker that had been sitting there invisibly. Converted all four to RGBA (color type 6) in place, preserving dimensions. Worth noting what this says about the verification argument: the bug was not subtle and not new, it was simply never on a machine that would complain.
- Added a macOS CI job as well, at your request. It needs no extra system packages — WebKit and Security.framework ship with the OS, unlike the GTK/webkit2gtk stack Linux needs. The Keychain round trip is attempted with no setup step, on the assumption that GitHub's runners keep the login keychain unlocked; if that assumption is wrong, that step is where it will show.
- Added store-level frontend tests against a mocked `invoke` (no new dependencies; `@testing-library/react` and jsdom are not installed). They lock the error-capture path, the `toolApproval` / `allowFileCreate` derivation from the permission preset, and `api_key_masked` passthrough including the literal `"not configured"`.
- Fixed context budget starvation. Packing was sequential greedy: the first section that did not fit was truncated, `remaining` went to zero, and **every later section was dropped** with "budget exhausted" — purely because of its position in the list, not its importance. One large active file or one large git diff could silence the project tree and the diff entirely. `services/context.rs` now assigns each section a priority and a share of the budget (`section_budget_rule`) and allocates in two passes (`allocate_with_quotas`): quota first, then unused allowance redistributed to sections that were truncated. A section granted under 240 bytes is excluded rather than filled with a truncation marker that carries no content.
- Fixed CJK token under-estimation, which mattered more than it looks for a tool aimed at Chinese developers. The estimate was `bytes / 4` everywhere, so a Chinese character (3 bytes) counted as 0.75 tokens when it is closer to 1 — a ~25% under-count. Combined with the greedy packing above, a Chinese prompt that fit the real budget could still be truncated. `estimate_tokens_for_text` now counts non-ASCII characters individually, and the token budget is converted to a byte budget using the measured byte-per-token ratio of the actual context (`bytes_per_token`, clamped to 2..4) instead of a fixed 4.
- Fixed the resumed-pipeline tool surface. `continue_agent_pipeline` called `attach_workspace_tools` and **discarded the returned invoker** (`let (llm, _) = ...`), so a resumed stage advertised workspace tools in the request body while tool calls were handled by whatever invoker happened to be left on the orchestrator from the previous run — or by nothing. It also never re-attached MCP tools, so a paused run silently lost them. The run's MCP policy is now remembered on `AgentOrchestrator::tool_policy`, and the resume path rebuilds both MCP and workspace tools and assigns `orch.tool_invoker`. Tool definitions and the executing invoker are now always attached together on all three entry points.
- Rewrote `docs/agent_ide_design.md` sections 3, 4.3, 5.2, 6, 8, 10, and 11 against the code. The document had drifted badly: section 8 still listed provider-native tool calling as "future work" when `stream_with_tool_loop` and the native `tools` transport had shipped, and sections 10-11 were ~450 lines of pseudo-code for designs this ROADMAP had already rejected (`IncrementalRenderer`, a standalone completion framework, a `ChinesePromptOptimizer` class, in-process `LocalModel` engines, an in-process plugin API, mobile support, real-time collaboration). Those are now recorded as decisions with reasons instead of being described as plans. Added a 4.3.1 section documenting the tool loop, the tool families, the 12-round bound, and the capability-degradation path.
- Gave the Agent a verification tool (9.0.9), which is the half of the loop that does not require touching the review gate. `workspace_run_command` lets the model run the project's own check commands and read the real exit code and output, instead of proposing a diff and waiting for a human to run the tests and paste the failure back. Design decisions worth recording, in the order the checks fire: (1) **Long-running commands are refused before the allow-list is consulted**, so listing `npm run dev` does not enable it — verification runs a command to completion and a dev server never exits, which makes this a safety invariant rather than a preference. (2) The allow-list is **derived by the backend from the project's own declared tasks** (`package.json` scripts, Cargo), not typed by the user as glob patterns and not chosen by the model. That keeps the boundary at "commands this project already defines" and required no new UI. (3) When `allowCommandRun` is false the tool is **neither advertised nor claimed by the invoker** — advertising a tool that always fails is worse than omitting it, because the model spends a round discovering that. (4) Output is tail-truncated, reusing the same `truncate_for_prompt` the repair prompt uses, because failures land at the end of output. (5) `is_command_allowed` / `normalize_command_pattern` moved from private `cli/mod.rs` functions into `services/verification.rs`, so the CLI's `--allow-run` and the tool's allow-list cannot drift into two different notions of "authorized" — the same extraction rule already applied to the repair prompt. This also makes `allowCommandRun` the third permission with real backend enforcement; `SECURITY.md` previously stated it had no effect on process execution, which is no longer true, and now also states the limits plainly (a permitted command is still arbitrary code execution by whatever the project declares; there is no environment or network isolation).
- Told the executor prompt to use the verification tool and to not claim a check passed without running it. A tool the model does not know to reach for is a tool that does not exist.
- Gave the Agent a write tool (9.0.8), which closes the observe/change/verify cycle inside a stage. `workspace_write_file` is advertised **only in `auto` mode**, and that gate is the whole design argument: `auto` already applies pending diffs without a human click, so writing during the run grants nothing it did not already have, while `suggest` / `edit` promise "review before it lands" and therefore do not get the tool at all. Whole-file replacement rather than patching, because hunk-location failure ("Could not find original content") is the main existing failure mode and the model already has a read tool to fetch exact content first; the cost is that it must preserve what it is not changing, which the tool description states. Creating a file additionally requires `allowFileCreate`, matching what Auto-mode apply already enforces. Deleting is deliberately not supported, so `allowFileDelete` stays honestly unenforced. The part that took the most thought: a raw write would **bypass the review area**, leaving the disk changed and the Diff view empty — which destroys the auditability this product exists for. So `AgentOrchestrator::record_tool_writes` publishes every write back as an `applied` diff carrying its pre-write content, plus an undo checkpoint, so `Undo Apply` covers tool writes too. Multiple writes to one file merge into a single entry whose `original` is the content from *before the run* rather than before the last write, because that is where undo has to return to. Publishing happens on **every** exit path including cancellation and failure — a write that landed before a stage failed is still on disk, and not recording it would be the same bypass by another route.
- The write log is shared through an `Arc` inside `WorkspaceToolPermissions`, so the copy handed to the tool and the copy remembered on the orchestrator are the same log. That is what lets `continue_agent_pipeline` publish writes from a resumed run rather than losing them.
- Fixed the LLM config never being loaded at startup. `fetchLlmConfig()` had exactly one caller — the Agent settings panel's mount effect — while the TopBar indicator reads `llmConfigured`, which starts `false`. So after launch the app claimed "LLM Not Configured" with a working config sitting unread in the backend, and opening the settings panel once "fixed" it. Same cause for two more symptoms: ChatView's profile dropdown was empty until the panel had been opened, and `chatProfileId` stayed null so prompts only worked because the backend falls back to the active profile. The new App.tsx effect is deliberately separate from workspace restore, which returns early when no workspace was ever saved — LLM config is global, and a first launch with no workspace still needs an accurate indicator. Not covered by a test: it is mount-time wiring, and verifying it needs component rendering that this project has no jsdom / `@testing-library/react` for (Phase 10.8).
- Added `agent_cli smoke ide-surface`, because an audit of the automation surface found a hole: `cli/mod.rs` imports **zero** `commands::` modules, so everything outside the Agent flow — the Git panel, the Commands panel, context packing — had no automated entry point at all and could only be checked by opening the app and clicking. The new subcommand probes those backends read-only through the same functions the desktop calls (`commands::git::git_status` / `git_diff` are plain functions with no `State`/`AppHandle`, so the CLI can call them directly), needs no LLM provider so it can run in CI, and writes `surface-probes.json`. Probes report `ok` / `unavailable` / `failed` as three distinct states rather than two: a workspace that is not a Git repository has nothing to show, which is not a defect, and collapsing that into "failed" would make the command permanently red in non-Git directories until nobody read it any more.
- **The new probe immediately found a real bug in the Git panel backend**: in a freshly `git init`-ed repository with no commits, `commands::git::git_status` returned `Err("HEAD: reference 'refs/heads/master' not found ... UnbornBranch")` because it called `repo.head()` unconditionally. So the desktop Git panel showed an error instead of a Git view for a brand-new project — a common first-run state. **Fixed (2026-09-07)**: both `git_status` and `git_diff` treat `UnbornBranch` as a state rather than an error. The decision the note asked for, recorded: `branch` is the name HEAD already points at (read from the symbolic ref, so a repo with no commits still reports `main` / `master` rather than `"HEAD"`), `entries` is the normal status list, and `ahead`/`behind` are `0` — not "unknown", but genuinely zero, since there is no local commit to compare. `git_diff` passes `None` as the tree, whose git2 semantics are "diff against the empty tree", which is exactly what `git diff` shows in that repository. The whole design rule was to match what the `git` CLI already does in the same repository, since that is the behaviour users expect. The probe's `UnbornBranch` tolerance was removed in the same change — keeping it would have silenced a regression as `unavailable`.

- Ran the probe binary against this checkout, which found a second real bug — this one in a safety invariant I had just written down. `is_long_running_command` did not recognise `cargo run`, so it appeared in the verification candidate list. For this repository `cargo run` launches the Tauri desktop app and never exits, so a `Verify All` pass — or the Agent's own `workspace_run_command` — would have hung until cancelled, which is exactly what that guard exists to prevent. `run` cannot simply be added to the keyword list: the check has a `contains` fallback, so it would also block `npm run test`. The fix matches only when the first two tokens are `cargo run`. Worth noting the shape of this: the guard was written, documented, tested — and still wrong, because the tests only covered the commands I thought of. Running the thing for real found it in one shot.
- Opened the frontend's mount-time blind spot (first slice of Phase 10.8). The startup bug above could not have been caught by any existing test: `fetchLlmConfig` itself was always correct, what was missing was anyone calling it on mount, and the suite had no way to observe mount behaviour at all. Added `jsdom` 30.0.1 and `@testing-library/react` 14.3.1 (pinned exact, `devDependencies` only), enabled per-file with a `// @vitest-environment jsdom` docblock rather than switching the global environment — the other ten test files run fine under node and there is no reason to move them. The two boot effects moved out of `App.tsx` into `hooks/useAppBootstrap.ts` so they can be exercised without rendering `App`, which would have required mocking Monaco, xterm and every lazy panel; four tests now cover mount-time config loading, loading it when there is no workspace to restore, workspace restore in the Tauri runtime, and survival when the workspace lookup rejects (a `.catch` path nothing had touched). **Verified the tests are not vacuous** by removing the `fetchLlmConfig` call and confirming all four fail. Note: `npm install` reports 10 pre-existing advisories in the dependency tree; `npm audit fix` was deliberately not run, since it is not part of this change.
- **Correction to several turns of my own advice**: I repeatedly said the desktop Agent flow had to be verified by hand in `npm run tauri -- dev`. That was wrong. `scripts/e2e/` already contains a full desktop E2E — `npm run e2e:workflow` builds the app, launches it against the `mock://workflow` provider, and drives it through UI Automation (run failing command → Problems → Fix → apply hunk → rerun → Git commit) with screenshots and file/Git assertions. I had listed `scripts/e2e/` in my first directory scan and never opened it.
- Ran it. It failed at `click-workflow: Element 'workflow' not found`, and **the same failure reproduces at `615402a`**, i.e. before any of this session's commits — so it is pre-existing, not a regression. The screenshots showed why: they contained unrelated windows, never the Agent IDE. The app window never reached the foreground.
- Root cause was in the harness, and it was the same class of defect as the `SetForegroundWindow` return value being discarded with `| Out-Null`. Windows silently refuses that call when another process owns the foreground, so the controller believed it had focus, clicked into the void, and blamed a missing element 30 seconds later. `workflow-controller.ps1` now reads `GetForegroundWindow` to confirm, and fails immediately at an `environment` step explaining that the harness needs an exclusive interactive desktop. Verified: the run now fails in seconds with that message instead of misattributing the problem.
- Also hardened two things found while fixing it: `Click-Element`'s fallback sends `{ENTER}` to whatever window holds focus — it had been landing in unrelated applications — so it now refuses unless the app really is foreground; and `Focus-App` returning a value meant its bare call inside `Wait-Element` leaked a boolean into that function's return value, which would have handed callers `[bool, element]` instead of an element.
- Documented the consequence in `docs/testing_strategy.md`: this E2E is a **manually triggered check on an idle machine**, not a regression net. It cannot run in CI (no interactive desktop) and cannot run on a machine in use (it takes over the foreground, mouse and clipboard). The automated net for backend behaviour is `agent_cli`.
- Closed the gap that mattered more than the harness itself: **nothing in the repository could drive the native tool loop**. Three separate reasons stacked up — the E2E profile uses `toolCallMode = "text_protocol"`, `stream_mock_chat` returns a plain `String` so the mock provider could not emit `tool_calls` at all, and `agent_cli` passed a `None` invoker. So `workspace_run_command` and `workspace_write_file` had unit tests for their own functions and zero evidence that a model calling them actually works. Now: `mock_tool_call` lets a `mock://` provider emit one tool call per stage on demand (`AGENT_IDE_MOCK_TOOL` / `AGENT_IDE_MOCK_TOOL_ARGS`, only for tools that were actually advertised, only until a `tool` message exists so it cannot spin to the 12-round cap), and `--allow-run` attaches the verification tool to CLI runs. `cli::tests::smoke_tool_loop_*` now drive the full chain headlessly with no provider and no desktop.
- The two new tests are a **differential pair** rather than a single happy path: identical runs differing only in `--allow-run`, asserting the command's side effect (a file written by the shell) exists in one and not the other. A diff-only assertion would have proved nothing — a failed tool call also feeds an error back and still produces a diff on the next round, so "the round completed" and "the command ran" are different claims. The negative case doubles as the check that an unauthorized tool is genuinely unreachable, not merely undocumented.
- Then closed the last tool with no end-to-end evidence: `workspace_write_file` now has a CLI grant, `--allow-agent-write`, and `cli::tests::smoke_write_tool_*` prove a model can write a file through the loop. Two decisions are worth recording. (1) It required a **new flag rather than reusing `--allow-edit` / `--allow-create`**: those govern whether produced diffs may be applied, and quietly promoting them to "the model may write files directly" is exactly the escalation the earlier note refused. `--allow-create` still only widens the grant to new files, and only alongside `--allow-agent-write`. (2) It **requires `--apply`**, mirroring the desktop's Auto-mode gate. The reasoning is the same in both places: a preview promises the workspace is untouched, and a write tool active during a preview breaks the only guarantee preview makes. The tests assert file contents on disk, not the tool's return string — a tool can report success without having written anything.
- Gave the CLI the equivalent of the desktop review area for those writes: `tool-writes.json` in the run's artifact directory. Without it, disk changed and nothing in the run record said which files or whether they were new. It is written whenever the flag is on, **even as an empty array**, because a missing file cannot distinguish "not authorized" from "authorized and never used".
- Found and fixed a false green while running the gate: `npm run e2e:workflow` copies the repo into `artifacts/e2e/workflow/<timestamp>/workspace/`, test files included, and Vitest's default `include` was running those copies — 43 test files where the repository has 10, with the extras being frozen snapshots of older code. A failure there would have pointed at history instead of at the working tree. `vite.config.ts` now pins `test.include` to `src/**`. `artifacts/` was already gitignored, which is why this stayed invisible: the suite looked bigger and stayed green.
- Started on the backend's largest untested file, `commands/agent.rs` (~1900 lines, zero tests of its own code — its one test module tested four functions defined in *other* modules). The conclusion after mapping it: it cannot be tested in place, because every interesting function takes `AppHandle` or `tauri::State`. So the work is to **move decisions out of it**, not to test it. Three moved this round, all with tests where they landed: `verify_workspace`'s candidate preparation and check-running loop → `services::verification::prepare_commands` / `run_checks` / `format_report_log`; the usage action-log wording → `RunUsageSnapshot::action_log_summary` / `action_log_details`; `resolve_context_compression` now takes the mutex it actually reads instead of `State<AgentGlobalState>`.
- Two rules came out of that, worth applying to the rest of the file. (1) A signature mentioning `State<T>` when the body reads one field is a testability bug, not a style question — dropping it made the compression-precedence rule testable with no fixture at all. (2) Wording that encodes a judgement belongs with the data, not the emitter: the "partially reported usage means the per-run cap undercounts" distinction *is* the point of that branch, and it was unreachable without `AppHandle`.
- One test had to be weakened to stay honest: `run_checks` maps a command that cannot launch into a failed check rather than aborting the batch, and the first version asserted `exit_code == None`. That is platform-specific — on Windows the shell reports a missing executable with a non-zero exit code instead of failing to spawn. The assertion now sits on the property that matters ("counted as a failure, earlier results survive") rather than on the shape of the exit code.
- Also removed a dead `#[tauri::command]` attribute on the private `local_model_status` (not in `invoke_handler`, so the attribute did nothing) along with its doc comment, which had been copy-pasted from `test_llm_connection` and described the wrong function.
- Then made the one invariant that was documented-but-unenforced structural instead. Three run commands have nine exit branches between them, and each had its own copy of "finish the run, register tool writes, write the usage log". They now all call one `finish_agent_run`. **Writing the helper immediately found the drift it was meant to prevent**: `run_agent_step`'s cancel and error branches never emitted the usage log, so a step cancelled or failed mid-run left no record of the tokens it had already spent — the tokens are spent either way, cancelling does not refund them. That bug is exactly what nine hand-copied sequences produce, and no test could have caught it because none of those branches is reachable without a running app.
- One exception is deliberate and commented: `run_agent_step` still registers tool writes *before* the match, because that has to happen before `record_step_success` or the tool-write diffs get counted into "this step produced N new diffs". The registration inside `finish_agent_run` is then a no-op — `take_writes` drains the log, so a second call finds nothing — which is what makes it safe to have both.
- Started 9.0.10 from the same angle: the CLI's bounded repair loop had its stop rule spread across an entry `if` and a `break` expression at the two ends of the loop body, which is the one part of that loop easy to get wrong (the body is just a call sequence). It is now `verification::RepairPolicy`, and the CLI drives itself from it — the existing CLI repair tests passing unchanged is the equivalence check. The four stop reasons are kept distinct rather than collapsed to a bool: checks passed, budget exhausted, diffs could not be applied, repair not enabled. Reading "repair failed" tells you nothing about whether to change the code, raise the budget, or go look at file permissions. Apply-failure is reported ahead of check-status because both stop the loop but only one needs a human.
- Also recorded there: repair requires apply. Without it the model's fix never reaches the workspace, so the re-run checks the *old* code and the loop burns its whole budget verifying nothing. That was already true of the CLI's flags; now it is a property of the policy, so the desktop cannot get it wrong independently.
- Then attacked the reason 9.0.10 could not be implemented testably in the first place: **the orchestrator held `AppHandle` directly**, so every method on it — including ones that only mutate state and send one notification — was unreachable from a test. `agent/events.rs` introduces `RunEvents`, a one-method trait for emitting to the frontend. `AppHandle` implements it, so no call site changed (`&AppHandle` coerces to `&dyn RunEvents`); tests pass `RecordingEvents` and assert what was emitted. Converted first: the leaf emitters (`emit_state`, `emit_pipeline`, `emit_step`, `emit_action_log`, `emit_review_action_log`). `run` / `continue_pipeline_from` still take an owned `AppHandle` because they spawn token-forwarding tasks; that is the next slice.
- Emission is worth asserting rather than treating as incidental: the frontend's entire state comes from these events, so a run that is logically correct but silent looks to the user like nothing happened. The first test written against the new seam pins the review action log's payload — level, phase, the `Diff Review` stage the frontend groups by, and a diff summary that actually names the file, plus the explicit `No reviewable diffs.` wording when there is nothing pending.
- Finished the migration in the same pass: `run` and `continue_pipeline_from` take `Arc<dyn RunEvents>` (owned, because they spawn token-forwarding tasks), and **`agent/orchestrator.rs` now imports no Tauri types at all**. The 2600-line file holding the pipeline no longer needs a desktop runtime to execute. Call-site churn was two lines — `Arc::new(app_handle.clone())` — because `AppHandle` implements the trait, so `&AppHandle` coerces at every other call site.
- Cashed that in immediately: a whole `run` against a `mock://` provider is now a plain `#[test]`. It asserts the plan, state and pipeline events actually reach the frontend, because those are three independent channels and a missing one leaves part of the UI frozen while the backend considers the run a success. That is the first automated coverage the pipeline has ever had, and the prerequisite 9.0.10 was waiting on — a repair loop can now be added to the orchestrator with a test that drives it.
- **9.0.10 done**: `AgentOrchestrator::repair_until_checks_pass` runs checks → repair prompt → apply → re-check, bounded by the shared `RepairPolicy`, exposed as the `repair_workspace` command. Decisions worth recording: (1) it is **gated on Auto mode**, not on a new permission — every iteration has to land on disk or the re-run tests the old code, and `suggest` / `edit` promise changes wait in the review area, so the request is refused rather than silently downgraded to a single round. (2) Fixes go through `apply_all_diffs` rather than writing files directly, so each round keeps its base-hash check and its undo point; a loop that leaves the workspace unrecoverable is worse than no loop. (3) The iteration budget is clamped to 3 — beyond that it mostly burns the budget, and each round is a real model call plus a real write. (4) Every iteration and the stop reason go into the action log, because otherwise the user sees a changed workspace with no record of how many attempts it took or why the Agent gave up.
- The two tests for it are the pair that matters rather than a happy path: one points the endpoint at a dead address to prove **the model is never called when the checks already pass** (a repair loop that always runs one round costs money and edits passing code), the other gives it a check that can never pass and asserts it stops at the budget with every iteration recorded.









Important distinction:

- `npm run dev`: Vite web preview only. Tauri IPC, filesystem, terminal, Git, and Agent backend are disabled or stubbed.
- `npm run tauri -- dev`: real IDE runtime with Rust backend and Tauri APIs.
